CNN and CBS News found Biden's final approval rating to be 37% and 36%, respectively.[^cite_note-886][^cite_note-887] Polling aggregator [FiveThirtyEight](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FiveThirtyEight) found that Biden had a final average approval rating of 37%.[^cite_note-888]

### Media depictions

See also: [*Saturday Night Live* parodies of Joe Biden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saturday_Night_Live_parodies_of_Joe_Biden)

Nine men have portrayed Biden on *[Saturday Night Live](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saturday_Night_Live)*, starting with [Kevin Nealon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kevin_Nealon) in 1991. [Jason Sudeikis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jason_Sudeikis) portrayed Biden during the 2008 election and reprised the role many times. During the 2020 election, Biden was played by [John Mulaney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Mulaney), [Alex Moffat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alex_Moffat), [Mikey Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mikey_Day), [Woody Harrelson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woody_Harrelson), and [Jim Carrey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jim_Carrey). In 2024, [Dana Carvey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dana_Carvey) played the role.[^cite_note-889][^cite_note-890] In 2016, [Greg Kinnear](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greg_Kinnear) portrayed Biden in the [HBO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HBO) television film *[Confirmation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confirmation_\(film\))*, about the [Clarence Thomas Supreme Court nomination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clarence_Thomas_Supreme_Court_nomination) hearings.[^cite_note-891]

Biden appears in episodes of *[Parks and Recreation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parks_and_Recreation)* during his time as vice president.[^cite_note-892] He appears as himself in a 2016 episode of *[Law & Order: SVU](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Law_&_Order:_Special_Victims_Unit)*.[^cite_note-893] *[The Onion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Onion)* ran a series of articles about a [fictionalized Biden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Biden_\(The_Onion\)).[^cite_note-894][^cite_note-895]

## Assessments

A December 2024 Gallup poll found that 54% of Americans thought Biden's presidency was below average or poor, 26% average, and 19% above average or outstanding.[^cite_note-896] Gallup found that Americans largely offered negative assessments of Biden's presidency on economic, national, and international issues. Of 18 issue areas Gallup tracked, a majority of Americans said the U.S. lost ground in six, including the economy in general, immigration, and the country's position in the world. A plurality found that the U.S. declined in six other areas, including national infrastructure and energy, education, and trade relations with other countries. A plurality felt the U.S. made progress in only one indicator under Biden: the conditions of [gay, lesbian, and transgender people](78174325.md).[^cite_note-897]

When Biden left office in 2025, journalists and many Democrats viewed his presidency as a failure due to [age and health concerns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Age_and_health_concerns_about_Joe_Biden), public frustration over inflation, and Harris's loss to Trump in the 2024 presidential election.[^cite_note-898] *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)* called the conclusion of Biden's presidency a "tragedy".[^cite_note-899]

### Historical assessments

Journalist [Amy Walter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amy_Walter), editor of the nonpartisan *[The Cook Political Report](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Cook_Political_Report)*, argued that the public deemed Biden's presidency a failure particularly due to frustration over inflation.[^cite_note-900] Economists debate the extent to which Biden's policies were responsible for inflation,[^cite_note-901] but according to Gallup, public perception of the economy in 2024 was worse only in 2008 and 1992, helping Trump win the 2024 presidential election.[^cite_note-902]

The [Siena College Research Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siena_College_Research_Institute)'s 2022 survey [ranked Biden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Historical_rankings_of_presidents_of_the_United_States#2022_Siena_College) 19th out of 45 presidents.[^cite_note-903] The 2024 *Presidential Greatness Project Expert* survey ranked Biden 14th.[^cite_note-bc-904] A February 2024 [American Political Science Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Political_Science_Association) poll of historians and scholars also ranked Biden the 14th-greatest president, diverging from public assessments. The pollsters noted that Biden's ranking was unusually high for a presidency without military victories or institutional expansion.[^cite_note-905][^cite_note-bc-904]

In 2026, historian [Julian E. Zelizer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julian_E._Zelizer) described Biden's presidency as similar to those of [George H. W. Bush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_H._W._Bush) and [Jimmy Carter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jimmy_Carter),[^cite_note-906] who were elected to office on a platform of change but served only one term.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZelizer2026-907]
